<a id="login"></a>
## 2. Log In & Device Approval

- **Route(s):** `/login`

### `apps/web/src/screens/auth/ApproveOnDevice.tsx` (28)

**Marker (line 1):** The new device's side of new-device approval (Plan 2 Task 16): the login waits until a device that is already logged in taps the same 2-digit number. It polls `POST /v1/login/approval/:id/poll` every 2 seconds (only this browser, with its approval cookie, can); `ok` means the session has started. Laid out to the LoginApprove board, side=new (C2-D1; docs/design-boards/LoginApprove.dc.html and -Wide on wip/design-brief): a full screen with the back control and "Log in", the number in a card, the countdown, "Where do I approve it?", the no-other-device card, and the actions in the footer. Design v2 batch 1 (4b355a56): the compact header, the 28/32 screen title, the number as the caution hero with a chip, the caution clock, the outline card, 56 px state tiles. All look comes from the UI kit (ui/FlowScreen, ui/Disclosure, ui.css). The board's new-device side is marked Draft copy: its new sentences here are COPY-UNSIGNED. Not built (new behaviour, see the C2-D1 report): the room note about the account's devices (review M5 keeps the account's devices off this screen), and the passkey / security key / PGP way in without an approval; the card keeps the existing link to the backup login.

### `apps/web/src/screens/auth/refusals.ts` (11)

**Marker (line 1):** What the login and security screens say when the server refuses something for a reason the person can act on (Task 30 re-review, items 2 and 3). One helper so every screen words a wait, a refused PGP key or an expired step the same way: - 429 `slow-down` / `locked`: how long to wait, from `retryAfterSeconds` (the TOTP backup's daily cap waits until midnight UTC, so a long wait is given as a clock time); - 400 `key-expired` / `key-revoked`: the key is turned away with the reason (spec 6.2); `key-no-encryption` / `key-encryption-inactive` (owner demo note #144): the key cannot receive the login code; - a `detail` sentence about the pasted key: shown as the server wrote it, among them a PGP login finish's 400 `key-weak` for a refresh that adds a part X2F doesn't accept (security review of #144 follow-up; the words are @x2f/accounts `inspectRefreshAdditions`'s, COPY-UNSIGNED); - 400 `challenge-expired`: the step ran out of time and must start again; - 409 `security-key-limit` / `login-name-set` (FU-A): the key cap, a login name already chosen. Anything else gets the caller's own fallback sentence.

| `file:line` | User-facing text |
|---|---|
| `apps/web/src/screens/setup/Login.tsx:111` | `PGP uses free, open-source encryption to lock and unlock messages with a cryptographic key pair: a public key you share with X2F, and a private key that stays strictly on your device. You will need a PGP app each time you log in to decrypt a login challenge, such as Kleopatra (Windows/Linux), GPG Suite (macOS), or PGPony (com.pgpony.android on F-Droid, our top Android pick) and OpenKeychain on Android. Because X2F never generates keys on your behalf, your private key never leaves your control.` |
| `apps/web/src/screens/setup/Login.tsx:115` | `Security Key` |
| `apps/web/src/screens/setup/Login.tsx:117` | `A security key is a small physical device, such as a YubiKey or a Nitrokey, that you plug into your computer or tap against your phone. When you log in, X2F asks the key to prove it is the one you registered, and you touch it and enter its PIN (or use its fingerprint) to confirm; X2F only accepts keys that check a PIN or fingerprint. The secret inside the key never leaves it, so nobody can copy it from a website or a leaked list. Keep a second key or another login method as a backup in case one is lost.` |
| `apps/web/src/screens/setup/Login.tsx:121` | `Passkey` |
| `apps/web/src/screens/setup/Login.tsx:123` | `A passkey is a login stored on your phone or computer and unlocked with your fingerprint, face or device PIN. Like a security key, it proves who you are without ever sending a secret that could be stolen or guessed. Passkeys are quick and easy to use, but some devices copy them to your Apple, Google or Microsoft account to sync them between devices, so pick a security key or PGP if you would rather nothing about your X2F login touches those accounts.` |
| `apps/web/src/screens/setup/Login.tsx:129` | `OTP means one-time password. You add X2F to an authenticator app, such as Aegis or 2FAS, by scanning a code once. From then on the app shows a new six-digit code every 30 seconds, and you type the current one when you log in. The codes come from a secret shared only between the app and X2F, and each one works only briefly, so a code someone sees over your shoulder is soon useless. On X2F an authenticator app counts as one of your two ways in, but it never logs you in by itself: you also need a PGP key, security key or passkey.` |

It follows one pretend sell trade from start to finish on example screens (FirstTradeStage.tsx), and the person watches the app being used. **Marker (line 1):** The team member's guided tour (owner 12 Nov 2026), as design specified it: docs/design-12nov/tour-stops.md §4 (wip/design-brief @b3b8315b). Data only: the tour engine (wip/lead-landing-tour) runs the steps and owns the shell and the motion (tour-motion.md; no 3D or isometric layer, which the owner rejected on 12 Nov). A stop shows only when the member's ticked boxes hold its ability ("Shows when"), read from `GET /v1/account/me`.`member.abilities` (`memberTour(abilities)`); `always` stops show to every member. Titles and card texts are design's, word for word (COPY-UNSIGNED, as there). The steps are the engine's own types (engine.ts; review 12 Nov, S2), grouped into its pages: design's arrow side "top" (card below) is `below`, "bottom" (card above) is `above`; on wide the engine puts the card beside the element, as design's "/ left" says. A member gets this tour once per device, the first time they open /sell, instead of the welcome screen and the trader's first-trade tour (lead, 12 Nov 2026; WelcomeGate.tsx). Design's stops whose screen does not work for a member on main are PENDING_MEMBER_STOPS, each saying what it waits for, and are in no tour until it does: Wallets and Settings → Security are owner-only screens for members (settings/OwnerOnly.tsx), My trades (/trades) has no tour page id yet and its tabs are not design's names, the dispute room needs the Example dispute design ruled for, the extend button and the replay row are not built, and Post an offer's stops are "as V1", which the rebuilt brief no longer has. The Trade room page needs a trade the member can open: `memberTour` takes its code, and leaves the page out without one. Every live target renders on its page as a member with that ability (test/memberTourSteps.test.tsx).

**Marker (line 1):** The help library (owner, 18 Nov 2026: "a help library of the questions people might ask, with answers"). One data file, so the words are easy to edit: the topics, then every question with its answer. COPY-UNSIGNED: every topic title, intro, question and answer below waits for design and the owner to sign it off. DESIGNER-REVIEW: the grouping and the order. Rules for this file (lead brief, 18 Nov 2026): - Every answer matches the code it was checked against: wip/lead-train-1 ef50b6e0 (which holds main 8e7e63d5) merged into this branch (review of 18 Nov). Each entry's `sources` lists where each fact lives, as `path:line` (or `path:first-last`) from the repository root, then a few words that are on that line. apps/web/test/helpLibrary.test.tsx checks that every entry has one and that its words are still in that file within 40 lines of the cited line (lead, 20 Nov: so adding lines to a cited file does not break it). Words that moved further but are in the file once only warn, naming the line to re-point the note to; words gone from the file fail. - An answer that names a time, a limit or a count lists it in `facts`; the same test rebuilds each fact's words from the constant in the code and fails when they differ or when an answer holds a number no fact covers. A number the owner can change in settings (the offer caps, the device limit, the appeal window) is said with "normally" in front of it (review M4). - Nothing the code does not do is promised. Undecided rules (who should bear the Monero network fee: the answers say only what payouts.ts does today, the owner's 16 Nov split since the netfee merge, liens and their recovery, the per-payment-method pay windows of owner p11-29, the window to dispute a refund) are left out or say plainly that the feature is "not in the app yet" (worded so, not "not available", so copy-lint does not read an explanation as a refusal). - An answer that depends on the proof-upload build flag (VITE_X2F_PROOF_UPLOAD, proof/flag.ts) has both answers in `proofUpload`; `libraryQuestions` picks one with the same `proofUploadOn` the trade screens use. - Plain words, 1 to 4 sentences, never "half" about fees.

**Marker (line 7):** "Trading with more than one person?" (build spec j31a section 2, owner p9-3 / T-039; boards BuyQuote, SellQuote: a bottom sheet; SellQuote-Wide: a centred dialog). Design final call (10 Nov 2026): it shows OVER the trade room the first time the taker's new trade opens (TradeRoom.tsx, quote/courtesy.ts), never on the quote before the room, so the browser's Back cannot lose the step into the room (review L2); on every new trade until they tick "Don't show this again" (kept on this device). Posters never see it, and there is no penalty for opening several trades (T-038). "Got it" is the only action and closes it; closing the sheet any other way does the same, since the trade is already open underneath. COPY-UNSIGNED

### `packages/trade/src/giftCards.ts` (29)

**Marker (line 1):** Gift-card rules (J-17; owner G50-G52 of 7 Nov, F13 and its decision T-081). Shared by the API (the post and take checks) and the web (the post form, the quote's tick-box terms and the trade room's evidence checklist). Only keys live here: every sentence a person reads is in the web's `giftCardCopy.ts`, COPY-UNSIGNED until design (J-31, T-058) writes the rules text. - G51: the card was bought within the last 30 days. - G52: the card carries its full written value. Only a range card (a Visa 200 to 500) may be partial, and then the sender must give video proof. - G50: the person holding the escrow has 45 minutes to check a card once its details are handed over (the gift-card method profile's confirm window, apps/api methods/profiles.ts). Video proof: the sender logs in to the card and shows it right before sending. - T-081: the card's currency follows its country; a short window; an evidence checklist; tick-box terms.

### `apps/web/src/screens/trade/CancelFinalSheet.tsx` (7)

**Marker (line 1):** "Cancelling is likely final" (board CancelRequest.dc.html on wip/design-brief, rule (c); owner G56 and g67, 7 Nov; build spec j31a section 3, owner p5-2 / T-049, 9 Nov: "likely" final, and the body names no one): the one warning before the buyer's own cancel. The buyer may cancel after the escrow is funded until they tap "I've paid", never after (then only a dispute); this sheet is the confirm step BuyerPay's quiet "Cancel trade" opens, and the buyer's wait for funding's. Two variants (design final call 10 Nov 2026): with coins held, the escrow-held version names what comes back ("The 1.250000 XMR in escrow is returned and this trade closes."); before anything is in the escrow (setup or funding, nothing received), the unfunded line "Nothing is in escrow yet, so nothing needs refunding." and no figure. "Keep trading" (the safe action) has focus; the danger-confirm "Cancel trade" goes on to `buyer-cancel`. Board wording, COPY-UNSIGNED (the owner signs it on the wording pages); the unfunded line is design's (10 Nov), COPY-UNSIGNED too.

### `apps/web/src/screens/trade/PaidWaiting.tsx` (43)

**Marker (line 1):** The buyer's room after "I've paid" (board BuyerPaidWaiting.dc.html and -Wide; design review 1 Nov 2026 shot 39, NEEDS_DESIGN "the after-I've-paid buyer state"): the status card says the trade is paid and the seller is checking, the timer shows the seller's confirm window, "What happens next" says what each side does, and the payment's details fold away under "Your payment". The footer has Nudge (the API's nudge on an open trade, spec 2.4), Open dispute (the terms list) (disabled with "Available from … in …" until the server's `confirmBy`: the payment method's window from I've paid, 90 min for instant ones, owner F9 7 Nov; either side waits it, owner p9-4; then the outlined button), and the reminder that a paid trade can't be cancelled. While trading is paused for maintenance the clock stops at the pause and no dispute is offered (owner Q10, G19). The status is the board's caution hero (chip, key mark, lead, then the timer inside it); once the window ends without a release its chip reads "Paid · not confirmed in time" (the board's window=ended). BuyerPay.tsx keeps the escrow side: Finish marking as paid, the refusals and the stopped escrow come in as `notices` and `primary`. Lines not on the board are COPY-UNSIGNED.

### `apps/web/src/screens/trade/Refunded.tsx` (43)

**Marker (line 1):** The buyer's refunded trade (board Refunded.dc.html, variants buyer-cancelled, time-ran-out and moderator; Plan 3 Task 27's RefundedSlot, moved here from dispute/DisputeSlot.tsx and filled out from the board, design review 1 Nov 2026 shot 48). The escrow went back to the seller; the screen says why (the hero: a chip and key mark, the headline and lead, the trade amount and "X2F fee: None"), what happened when ("What happened": the timeline from the trade's own times), and, while the buyer may still dispute the refund (48 hours after it, owner 2026-10-29; never after a cancel, owner 2026-10-29 question 4), the board's dispute card. The frame is the trade room's own (.x2f-pay). The rules are Task 27's, unchanged: `disputeEntry` decides whether a dispute can still be opened, the reasons are `refundReason`'s (dispute/rules.ts, the dispute page keeps using it), and a buyer's cancel reads "Cancelling" until the seller confirms the XMR is back (Task 39). The seller is "the seller", never named, on refund and cancel results (owner 2026-10-29; lead 8 Nov: the owner's rule beats the design session of 2 Nov). Lines not on the board are COPY-UNSIGNED.

### `apps/web/src/screens/trade/SellerCancelConfirm.tsx` (19)

**Marker (line 1):** The seller's side of a buyer's cancel (owner 2026-10-29: option B with a seller confirm step). The buyer cancels before "I've paid" and the XMR goes back to the seller's locked refund address at once (the buyer's app and the moderation service sign; the seller need not be online). The trade says "Cancelling" until the seller taps Confirm cancel, which stays greyed out until the returned XMR has all its confirmations (10, the count the trade uses for funding and release); the API checks the count itself on its own node (`confirm-cancel`, too-early before 10). If the seller never taps, the trade stays among their active trades with "Your input is needed to cancel this trade." and closes as cancelled by itself SELLER_CONFIRM_CANCEL_TIMEOUT_H (48) hours after the button became available (`cancelConfirmableAt`; the server's timer, owner 2026-10-29). No board draws this screen: COPY-UNSIGNED.

### `apps/web/src/screens/trade/SellerCancelled.tsx` (9)

**Marker (line 1):** SellerCancelled (owner 8 Nov, p6-1, p9-2; replaces Task 25's FinalAlert): the buyer's screen while the seller's cancel refund settles (`settling` as `auto-refund`). The time to pay ended before the buyer tapped "I've paid" and the seller cancelled, so the XMR is going back to the seller at once: there is no final warning and nothing for the buyer to sign. A buyer who paid late anyway can open a dispute at once (the machine allows the buyer's `open-dispute` here). In the usual case it does not stop the refund: the moderator co-signs within seconds of the cancel and a refund already handed out still lands, so the case goes on as an after-refund dispute (the seller is asked to send the XMR as agreed; review I1, 8 Nov). Only a dispute opened before the moderator's pass keeps the coins in escrow. With no dispute, once the refund lands the trade is `refunded` and Task 27's RefundedSlot takes over (its 48-hour dispute window). COPY-UNSIGNED: no board draws this screen yet.

### `apps/web/src/screens/trade/SellerRefund.tsx` (34)

**Marker (line 1):** SellerRefund (board SellerRefund.dc.html, plan Task 25; owner 8 Nov, p6-1, p9-2, p9-4): the seller's screen once the buyer's time to pay has ended without "I've paid". Three views: - **Time to pay ended** (`secured`, past `payBy` by the server clock, `sellerRefundOpen`): the board copy, the dispute-after-refund warning (spec 2.3, shown before the tap), then **Cancel and get refund** (the one primary): `request-refund`, the `trade.refund-request` signal, then `buildRefund()` (refundFlow.ts). The refund starts at once: no final warning to the buyer and no nudge needed. Then Give 30 more min and Nudge, which the screen offers and encourages (it often saves starting a new trade) but never requires; one per 15 min, as many as needed. Until the seller cancels, the buyer can still tap "I've paid" (the time to pay is not a hard end); once they have, this view is gone (the trade is `paid`). - **Refund in progress** (`settling` as `auto-refund`): the refund is being signed and sent. A run this page started and did not finish (the signal or the tx set) is resumed here on mount; after a reload the view sends the signal and asks for the tx set again (`refundResume`). When it fails the seller sees why and Retry (review 2026-10-27 I1). The refund is seller-built (builder seller, co-signer mod, Task 25): the moderator can co-sign only the tx set THIS device builds, after the two have swapped fresh multisig info, so the refund waits for this device until the moderator reports it sent (`handedOff`, its signed DONE). Until then the view says to keep the page open, and that a closed page only pauses the refund: opening the trade again on this device resumes it (gap 4, 20 Nov: the e2e saw the refund never land once the page was closed early). The page may close once this device saw the refund in a block (`done`; review M1). - **Refunded** (`refunded` as `auto-refund`): the refund's confirmations from X2F's own node; the trade is complete once it has all 10 (owner 8 Nov). Every button asks the @x2f/trade machine again on the server clock (refundRules.ts). The header's Chat link is the board's; it points at the room's chat anchor. COPY-UNSIGNED: the board predates the owner's 8 Nov reversal; the lead and nudge lines, and the refund-in-progress and refunded views, have no signed copy yet.

**Marker (line 333):** What the refund needs from this device (gap 4 and its security review, 20 Nov). The refund is built by this device: - `signing` (or ready) before `handedOff`: keep the page open; a closed page pauses the refund until the trade is opened again on this device. The browser asks before leaving (stillNeeded). - `handedOff` (the moderator's signed report that it sent the refund, not yet seen in a block): keep the page open a few minutes while it confirms (review M1 a, m2). - `done` (this device saw its own refund in a block): the page may close. - `idle` (review m6): this device does not hold the trade's escrow, so it is not needed here: no warning, and the room's own "not on this device" line. - stopped: waiting for this device (retryable), or stopped for good (an integrity error, review m5). **Marker (line 638):** Whether `text` is a rendering of `pay`'s values (review F3): the title, then one "&lt;label>: &lt;value>" line per filled box, in order, with each value exactly as sealed. The labels themselves are not checked: they are the table's COPY-UNSIGNED wording, which the owner re-words (J-26), and a package sealed under older labels must still open. A label is 1 to 120 characters with no colon and no line break (review R1, belt and braces). Read from the end: a value may span lines (a mailing address), a label never does, so each label is the rest of its line, and the split is unambiguous. A reader never shows the sender's label text: once this passes it replaces the text with its own renderPayDetailsText (review R1: a label could otherwise name an account that is in no box).

| `file:line` | User-facing text |
|---|---|
| `packages/trade/src/payFields.ts:652` | `: ${pay.fields[i]!.value}` |

**Marker (line 1):** Owner p11-16 (8 Nov), drawn by design (BuyerPay tweak `dispute: opened`, docs/to-lead.md 9 Nov, ea2a9ca8): a dispute opened before "I've paid" greys out the payer's "I've paid" at once, with a line under it and an alert sheet that opens at once. The payer gets it whoever opened the dispute. The API already refuses "I've paid" on a disputed trade (the machine's mark-paid is wrong-state outside `secured`); this is what the payer sees. **Marker (line 134):** The buyer's refunded view says why the XMR went back to the seller, for every kind of refund (owner 2026-10-29; the seller is "the seller", never named): the buyer cancelled; the payment time ran out and the seller cancelled; a dispute decision; both agreed to cancel; the funding time ran out. Until trades record `refund_reason` (NEEDS_REFUND_REASON) the reason is inferred. A trade that had a dispute was refunded by its decision (a moderator's refund to the seller is the `dispute-seller` set, Plan 4 Task 15, so the dispute decides the reason, not the kind). **Marker (line 1):** Devices and sessions (Task 30, spec §6.3; Plan 2 Task 16 for the limit and the approval rule; Plan 2 Task 18 for team members' sessions): its own screen at /settings/devices, linked from Settings → Security. The count against the limit with a meter, your devices with remote log-out (asked first), "Log out all other devices" (your own devices only), team members' sessions in their own card with "Log out all team members" (asked first; owner, via the Devices board), the "One device at a time" switch (confirm-it's-you), this session's length ("Stay logged in for", owner 2 Nov 2026) and how a new device gets in. The current device is marked and cannot log itself out here (that is the Log out button elsewhere). Laid out to the "Devices and sessions" board (C2-D1; docs/design-boards/Devices.dc.html and -Wide on wip/design-brief); all look comes from the UI kit. Words: `C` below (COPY-UNSIGNED). The count is the API's: a team member's session counts toward the limit (`devicesInUse`), so the screen counts it too.

### `apps/web/src/screens/account/AccountFrozen.tsx` (23)

**Marker (line 1):** AccountFrozen (D-016, build spec j33a section 6; boards AccountFrozen(+Wide) on wip/design-brief, new 9 Nov): the full-screen notice that a moderator froze trading on the account. Laid out like Ban-Notice with a caution hero: the facts (since, no end date, the dispute it came from), what still works, what is paused. "Go to your dispute" is the one primary; Close and "Not now" go home. Every string is COPY-UNSIGNED. The wording matches My offers' hold notice. The frozen state comes from `GET /v1/account/freeze` (D-013..D-016: a moderator froze the account as a party who ignored a dispute or a ruling; no end time; a moderator lifts it once the person has done what the dispute asks, which they tell the moderator in the dispute chat). FROZEN_PATH reads it and goes home while the account is not frozen. `FrozenWatch` opens the notice once per freeze in this browser session after a login (the account owner's sessions; a team member's login never asks). The Jabber alert is the generic account-change notice the freeze row already sends (jabber/changePaths.ts). Not wired here: opening the notice from a refused take or post (those show their CantDoThat words).

The kind is not on the board and has no choice (design build review 7 Nov): a draft keeps its kind, a new message is feedback. **Marker (line 1):** News (Plan 4 Task 28; owner, 2026-10-29; spec 6.6): messages from X2F, newest at the top, each with the date it was posted, so a reader can tell new from already seen. It opens after login when something is new (authApi.afterLogin) and works logged out, because the emergency-stop message sends everyone here ("Check the News section"). Laid out from the News board (origin/wip/design-brief docs/design-boards/News.dc.html; design review 1 Nov shot 60) with UI-kit parts only, no stylesheet of its own (NEEDS_DESIGN_NEWS still waits for the board in design/x2f-app): Close News, "3 new · 4 posts", Mark all as read, "Newest first", collapsible posts (a new one on surface with the escrow edge and "New", a read one outlined with "Read"), dates in the one format (ui/dates.ts). Board copy is used as drawn; the rest is // COPY-UNSIGNED. New versus seen is kept on this device only (news-model.ts). Messages that were new when the page opened keep their "New" mark for this visit; opening the page marks them seen for next time. "Don't show me this again" (design session 2 Nov) shows on the News page that opened by itself after a login (`?after=login`), and is kept on this device only (news-model.ts).

**Marker (line 1):** W-006 (owner 28 Oct Q6 final: "phone push + loud alarm sound (default; custom sound file + volume) definitely"; Plan 7 Task 16, P7-A11): the optional loud alarm. - Off until the person turns it on. Default volume 80%; the default sound is a tone made on the device (Web Audio), so there is no sound file to fetch. - "Choose sound": an audio file of at most `ALARM_MAX_BYTES`, kept on this device only (IndexedDB), never uploaded: nothing here makes a request. - Which pushes ring it: the kinds sw.js can tell apart (`AlarmEvent`). Lead default (P7-A11 reads "the final alert and payment marked"; both arrive as the trade push today): trade changes, the seller's cancel input and the owner's alert ring; nudges do not. The person can change that. - On the web it rings while an X2F tab is open: sw.js posts `{ type: 'x2f-push', kind }` to open windows and `AlarmListener` plays. With no tab open the push is the normal notification. The Android app's alarm channel (rings on silent) is Plan 6 Task 20 and not built here. Settings live in this browser's storage (`x2f.alarm`), like the device's own sound: they are per device by design (a phone and a laptop want different alarms). Words are COPY-UNSIGNED (Plan 7 Task 8's Notifications board is not drawn).

| `file:line` | User-facing text |
|---|---|

### `apps/web/src/ui/margin.ts` (6)

**Marker (line 1):** One form for an offer's price against the market, everywhere a margin is shown (design, final call 10 Nov 2026): "0.4% above market", "0.5% below market", "at market" at exactly 0. Up to two decimals, trailing zeros trimmed ("0.4%", never "0.40%"; a typed hundredth stays: "0.03%", "12.25%"). Every offer is the market ± % (owner Q1), so this is the whole price line: the offer card (browse/OfferCard.tsx), the quote's bands card (quote/QuoteBands.tsx), Post an offer's preview (PostOffer.tsx) and My offers (myoffers/model.ts) all route through here, never through a formatter of their own. Whole-number maths on basis points; nothing goes through a float. test/margin.test.ts. COPY-UNSIGNED: "above market", "below market", "at market".

### `apps/staff-web/src/screens/ModQueue.tsx` (30)

**Marker (line 36):** The moderator's queue (Plan 4 Task 18, board ModQueue): the header naming the first-reply time, "Take next case", four tiles, the filter chips with their counts, one row per open case with its deadline bar, approval chip and assignee, and the footer naming the large-trade limit. The counts on the chips are of the rows shown (the API's first 500, closest deadline first); the tiles are the API's counts over every open case. The board's waiting rows say how long the first reply took ("Replied in 38 min"); the queue answer does not carry that time, so a replied row says "Replied" with no bar. DESIGNER-REVIEW. Sentences the board does not word are COPY-UNSIGNED. Mod polish (no board; DESIGNER-REVIEW, COPY-UNSIGNED): two filters beside the chips, by status and by how long a case has waited (over 1 hour, over the first-reply time, over 24 hours); a count badge of the rows shown; and the oldest-waiting timer, the case that has waited longest for its first reply against the first-reply setting (`settings.firstResponseH`), ticking each 30 seconds from the API's `waitingMs`.

### `apps/staff-web/src/screens/MySecurity.tsx` (74)

**Marker (line 27):** My security (lead re-review 6 Nov items 1 and 2: staff and the owner are refused on the trader site, so their own account work is here; perm `self`, every role, one's own account only, from apps/api/src/staff/account.ts). Three cards, as the trader app's Settings → Security, Devices and Alerts screens lay them out, built from the staff app's own kit: - Login methods: each method with when it was added and last used. A PGP key, a security key or a passkey can be removed with a `remove-method` tap of one of the person's security keys (the owner taps both owner keys, owner G4); the API keeps the login rule (the owner's two keys, a moderator's PGP key and passkey) and says why when it refuses. A PGP key can be added: paste it, decrypt X2F's message, then an `add-pgp-key` tap bound to the fingerprint the API read (shown first, to compare with the PGP app). Security keys and passkeys are added through the owner (a reset link), which the card says. - Sessions: every live session on both sites; any other one can be logged out (asked first; no tap, since it only takes power away). This session is marked and is logged out with Log out. - Alerts: the newest 50, unseen first marked New. "Mark as seen" is the person's own act and is logged (lead review 7 Nov M1), so the page never marks them by itself. No board draws it (DESIGNER-REVIEW). Words: COPY-UNSIGNED (all of C).

### `apps/staff-web/src/screens/settings/FeeSweepSection.tsx` (25)

**Marker (line 10):** B-006 / Plan 4 Task 9, reworked by the owner on 26 Nov 2026: half of every 1% goes to the hot payroll wallet, which pays staff automatically at clock-out; anything it holds over the owner's cap moves to the watch-only fee wallet, whose spend key stays offline (`GET`/`PUT /staff/v1/fee-sweep`, apps/api/src/staff/feeSweep.ts). The three values are saved together with a `fee-sweep` tap bound to exactly them; an empty cap turns the sweep off. The checks mirror `parseFeeSweepChange`: amounts in XMR to the atomic unit, a cap needs the amount to keep and the fee wallet's address and must be above the amount to keep; the API checks the address is a valid standard address on its network (400 `address-invalid`) and is X2F's fee wallet (400 `not-fee-wallet`). Without a connected payroll wallet (`walletWired` false) the section says so. NEEDS_DESIGN_FEE_WALLET: no board draws it. Words: COPY-UNSIGNED (all of C).

### `apps/staff-web/src/screens/StaffList.tsx` (27)

**Marker (line 27):** The owner's Staff page (Plan 4 Task 17, board StaffList; perm staff.manage). The add panel makes an invite: role card, login hours, then "Create invite link", which takes a `create-invite` tap bound to a token this browser makes (api.ts `createInvite`) and shows the link once with "Copy link". The table lists everyone the API returns, the owner first; the owner's own row has no action (the API refuses every change to the owner too, 409 `owner-protected`). The actions, as the API takes them (apps/api/src/staff/members.ts, invites.ts): - Switch off: at once, no tap (it only removes power; the plan's Task 7); - Switch on: a `switch-on` tap bound to the person and their role; - Cancel invite: no tap; - Change (board: "You can change these any time from their row"; not drawn, DESIGNER-REVIEW): the role (a `set-role` tap), the login hours (no tap, as the API takes them), Remove from staff (a `remove-staff` tap, owner G4) and, for the owner only, Reset this member's security (D-032, owner G60: a typed-name confirm that says exactly what happens, then a `reset-staff-security` tap bound to a re-enrolment link this browser makes, shown once). Below the table, the per-person checkboxes (owner Q60, J-21; StaffGrants.tsx, not drawn). Board copy is verbatim. Copy the board does not have is draft. COPY-UNSIGNED

### `apps/staff-web/src/screens/StaffLog.tsx` (38)

**Marker (line 24):** Plan 4 Task 19: the Activity log page (board StaffLog). The owner reads every entry; a senior moderator or a moderator reads the staff scope (disputed trades only, never staff, settings, owner, site, pay or news entries: the API decides). Filters: the trade code (with or without dashes, board), the Active disputes list (board), and, not on the board (DESIGNER-REVIEW), who acted, the kind of entry and a date range. 50 entries a page, "Show older entries". The owner also sees the moderation process's log alarm (A8's sentence, Task 17's log-alarm card) and the chain check. Review m7: the page load reads only the entry count and the alarm; the chain check (the whole log re-hashed by the API from its first entry) runs when the owner taps "Check integrity". The Active disputes list is paged (100 at a time, "Show more"). Copy beyond the board's is draft. COPY-UNSIGNED

### `apps/staff-web/src/tour/steps.ts` (138)

**Marker (line 1):** The staff app's guided tours, one per role (owner 12 Nov 2026), as design specified them: docs/design-12nov/tour-stops.md §3 (wip/design-brief @b3b8315b). Data only: the tour engine (wip/lead-landing-tour) runs the steps and owns the shell and the motion (tour-motion.md: the scrim, ring, arrow and card; no 3D or isometric layer, which the owner rejected on 12 Nov). Titles and card texts are design's, word for word (COPY-UNSIGNED, as there), but for S5 and the Clock out and Notifications stops (review 12 Nov, S1: lead drafts, COPY-UNSIGNED, for the owner). The steps are the engine's own types (apps/web tour/engine.ts, through ui/kit.ts; review S2): design's arrow side "left" (the staff web is wide-first: the card right of the element) is `auto`, which puts the card beside the element on a wide screen; "top" (card below) is `below`; "bottom" (card above) is `above`. A tour is the shell stops (S), then each page in the person's menu in menu order: the menu the server sends, `GET /staff/v1/me`.`nav` (review S3), so the tour is the menu even when the server narrows it (login hours, a revoked grant). Next / Next screen / Finish follow from the pages. Design's stops on pages or parts that are not built on main yet (ModQueue, ModCase, StaffPerformance, StaffLog, StaffSettings' pay, fee wallet and owner keys, Overview's approvals, the past-reputation check, the session countdown shown in tour mode) are PENDING_STAFF_STOPS: named by board, never in a tour until their screen exists, when each gets its selector and moves into PAGE_STEPS. Every live target renders on its route (test/tourSteps.test.tsx).

### `apps/staff-web/src/ui/ClockOut.tsx` (21)

**Marker (line 27):** The clock-out sheet (Plan 4 Task 33, D-041 and D-045; owner 2026-10-29 lead calls q5): the transparent total of every dispute completed since the last paid clock-out, a thank-you, and the pre-approved address to send it to (confirm the shown one or pick another; adding one is a security-key tap). "Clock out" is the only way staff are paid: logging out or closing the app is not a clock-out. A clawback debt or a missing price pays nothing now and waits for the next clock-out. **Marker (line 1):** The Terms step of the staff invite (Plan 4 Task 16; owner 9 Nov 2026, p11-14: "everyone who gets a login signs", staff too). The same parts as the trader app's Terms screen (apps/web screens/setup/Terms.tsx), never a copy of its text: the key points, the draft note and the full text come from the trader app's termsText.ts through the kit, the version from `GET /staff/v1/terms` (the staff origin's answer to the trader API's `GET /v1/terms`: the same `X2F_TERMS_VERSION`), and the signature pad is the trader app's SignaturePad (design 9 Nov, p11-15, canvas ea2a9ca8: drawn, or the typed name for anyone who can't draw). The scroll box must be read to its end before the tick box enables; Continue needs both the tick and a signature. A server version this bundle does not carry is not accepted from here (the trader's review M4): the stale notice asks for a reload. `onAccept` gets the version and the signature; the invite page sends them with the names (`acceptedTerms`, `termsSignature`). `changed` is set when the API refused those with `terms-not-accepted` (the Terms changed between reading and accepting, the trader's review M3): the step is mounted again, empty, and says so. The trader's sentences are reused where they are the same; the step's own lines (the heading's owner passes it, the intro, "Continue") are draft copy. COPY-UNSIGNED

### `apps/web/src/screens/settings/AbilityPicker.tsx` (2)

**Marker (line 1):** Job 1c (J-10; spec 6.7; owner S-087, S-092, S-094, S-097): the permission boxes, for an invite and for a member's edit. Drawn from the API's own catalogue (`GET /v1/team/permissions`), never a copy: its groups (each a Disclosure with how many boxes are ticked), its presets as buttons that fill the boxes, and each box with what it lets the member do. A box the catalogue marks `not-built`, `waits-member-keys` or `waits-member-chat-key` can be ticked like any other and says "Coming later" (the API stores it; it does nothing until the feature is built). The owner's alone ones say "Only you" and the always-on ones "Every member has this": those two cannot be ticked, and `sendable` drops them from what is sent. No Claude Design board yet (NEEDS_DESIGN_TEAM): kit parts and existing classes only, no inline styles. COPY-UNSIGNED: every word here is in teamCopy.ts (PICKER_COPY) or the catalogue's own.

### `apps/web/src/screens/support/Tickets.tsx` (3)

**Marker (line 1):** Support tickets (Plan 4 Task 31; spec 6.6, Helper): the trader's list of their own tickets (/support/tickets) and "Open a ticket" (/support/tickets/new), reached from Help, Support. The trader picks a category ("Everything else" last), may name one of their own trades, writes, and follows the thread (TicketThread.tsx). API: apps/api/src/tickets/traderRoutes.ts. No board yet (Task 31's controller step, "ticket boards for traders and staff", has not run): built from kit parts only (FlowScreen, ChoiceGroup, StatusChip, EmptyState, Notice, Button, CantDoThat) and the trades list's row styles. DESIGN: not drawn, DESIGNER-REVIEW; every word is COPY-UNSIGNED (tickets-model.ts). Refusals: TICKET_OPEN_AT_ONCE tickets not closed (409 `too-many-tickets`) and a team member (403 `owner-only`: tickets are the account owner's) open CantDoThat; the write gap and the daily cap are said inline, as the feedback form says its limit.
